- [[APIGen]] — APIGen generates function-calling training data and keeps only calls that pass format, execution and semantic checks; xLAM-7B trained on its output ranked 6th on BFCL (85.65 overall, June 2024 snapshot, self-reported).
- [[Arcade Learning Environment]] — The Arcade Learning Environment wraps the Atari 2600 emulator as an RL environment with a train/test split over games; its 2013 paper benchmarks five linear Sarsa(lambda) agents and two search agents on 55 games, and search won most games (self-reported).
- [[Autodata Agentic Self-Instruct]] — Autodata wraps data creation in an agent loop that accepts a question only when a weak solver fails and a strong solver succeeds; the resulting reinforcement-learning data beat single-pass data in three domains, though about half of one gain was truncation fixing.
- [[AutoEnvScaling Terminal Environment Design]] — AutoEnvScaling has a proposer agent build training environments as a terminal task inside a sandbox, validate them with a reference solution and an empty solution, and calibrate difficulty from solver results; it raised valid-environment rates and Terminal-Bench 2.1 scores over a static pool (self-reported).
- [[Batch Normalization]] — Batch Normalization (Ioffe & Szegedy, 2015) normalizes layer inputs using mini-batch statistics during training to reduce internal covariate shift — enables higher learning rates, reduces sensitivity to initialization, and acts as a regularizer. Rethinking BatchNorm (Wu & Johnson, 2021) exposes pitfalls with EMA population statistics, train/inference inconsistency, and domain shift.
- [[BeaverTails Decoupled Safety Preference Dataset]] — A dataset that annotates harmlessness and helpfulness separately on red-team QA pairs, with a 14-category safety meta-label, used to train reward and cost models and a PPO-Lagrangian Safe RLHF run on Alpaca-7B.
- [[BitFit Bias-Term Fine-Tuning]] — BitFit freezes every weight matrix of a BERT-style encoder and trains only its bias vectors plus the classifier, matching full fine-tuning on GLUE averages with under 0.1% of the parameters (self-reported).
- [[Black-Box Tuning for Language-Model-as-a-Service]] — Black-box tuning optimises a continuous prompt with CMA-ES in a random 500-dimensional subspace using only forward passes of a hosted model, with no gradients or weights (self-reported 73.01 average on 7 tasks, 16-shot).
- [[BM25 and the Lexical Gap]] — BM25 ranks documents by saturated, length-normalised term matches; both it and TF-IDF cosine are blind to synonyms, which is the gap dense encoders and hybrid retrieval fill.
- [[Bradley-Terry Preference Model]] — The pairwise-comparison model that converts human choices between two responses into a scalar reward — the shared formal core underneath RLHF reward models, DPO, and RLAIF preference models.
- [[CALM Composition to Augment LLMs]] — CALM joins a frozen large anchor model to a frozen smaller specialist through learned projections and cross-attention over every fourth layer; the authors report gains on low-resource translation, maths and code over the anchor without the forgetting seen after further pre-training, and a synthetic task neither model can do alone (self-reported).
- [[Chain of Hindsight]] — Chain of Hindsight fine-tunes a language model on sequences that pair outputs with templated 'Bad' and 'Good' feedback using the ordinary next-token loss, so no RL stage is needed; on GPT-J 6B it beat PPO-based RLHF in pairwise human preference.
- [[Chain-of-Agents AFM Distillation]] — Chain-of-Agents distils trajectories of a multi-agent system into one agent foundation model with supervised fine-tuning and then DAPO reinforcement learning, reporting 59.8 on AIME25 and 55.3 on GAIA for 32B models (self-reported, industry team, small-sample evaluations).
- [[ClawGym II Black-Box Harness RL]] — RL that trains a model through an existing agent harness treated as a black box (OpenClaw or Claude Code), recording token ids and logprobs at a serving proxy and rebuilding trajectories as a prefix tree; the paper reports gains on ClawGym-Bench over the initial policy (self-reported).
- [[Cognitive Behaviors for Self-Improving Reasoners]] — Gandhi et al.'s account of why one base model improves under RL on a verifiable task while another plateaus: four behaviours (verification, backtracking, subgoal setting, backward chaining) in the initial policy, tested on Countdown with two 3B models.
- [[Compiling Workflows into Weights]] — Dennis et al. write a procedure as a flowchart, have a strong model generate dialogues along its paths, and fine-tune a small model on them so no orchestrator runs at inference; they report near-in-context quality at 128-462x lower cost per conversation on three scripted customer-service domains (self-reported, judged by the teacher model).
- [[Composer 2 Training Recipe]] — Cursor's coding model: continued pretraining of Kimi K2.5, then RL in the production harness with Dr. GRPO-style updates, self-summarization trained end to end and a length penalty; scored on the internal CursorBench-3.
- [[Computer Use Visual Grounding]] — How a GUI agent turns a screenshot into a click — Set-of-Mark labelling, structured DOM/accessibility indexing (prefer it whenever structure exists) and direct coordinate prediction with aspect-ratio-matched rescaling — plus the observation-interface fix for dynamic screens and why per-step efficiency is now the bottleneck.
- [[ComputerRL API-GUI Agent RL]] — ComputerRL combines an action space mixing generated APIs with GUI actions, step-level GRPO with a binary outcome reward, and Entropulse, which alternates RL with SFT on collected successes to restore entropy; its 9B agents scored 48.9% on OSWorld (self-reported).
- [[Constitutional AI and RLAIF]] — Anthropic's two-phase replacement for human preference labels — critique-and-revise SL-CAI followed by RLAIF against a written constitution — plus what AI feedback provably matches and where it still underperforms humans.
- [[Continued Pretraining for Domain Adaptation]] — Continue a pretrained model's language-model training on domain text (DAPT), on the task's own unlabelled text (TAPT), or both, before fine-tuning; Llemma applies the recipe at 7B and 34B to turn Code Llama into a mathematics model (self-reported).
- [[Contrast-Consistent Search]] — Contrast-Consistent Search (CCS) trains a linear probe on a frozen model's unlabeled hidden states so that a yes-no statement and its negation get opposite probabilities; Burns et al. report it beating calibrated zero-shot on average over 5 models and 10 datasets, and holding up under a misleading prefix (self-reported).
- [[Critical-State RL]] — Critical-State RL picks which single tool call in a multi-turn trace to train on, ranking candidates by how much the action itself changes the outcome, then runs occurrence-local RL on that call only; it beat the alternative turn in all four BFCL cells tested (self-reported).
- [[DAPO]] — Four changes to PPO/GRPO-style RL — Clip-Higher, token-level loss, dynamic sampling and overlong reward shaping — each aimed at stopping the policy from collapsing early onto a single strategy; the reported state-of-the-art recipe for verifiable-reward math RL.
- [[DECKARD LLM-Hypothesized World Model]] — DECKARD asks a few-shot LLM once for a crafting-recipe dependency graph, then verifies and corrects it through Minecraft experience during RL training, so the LLM guides exploration but not execution; it cut steps to a stone pickaxe from 32M to 2.6M.
- [[Deep Q-Network, Target Network and Experience Replay]] — A Deep Q-Network fits a neural network to the Bellman target and keeps that training stable with a lagged target copy, a replay table sampled at random and stacked frames; a textbook chapter implements it on Breakout and contrasts it with a policy-gradient agent on CartPole.
- [[DeepSearch MCTS in RLVR Training]] — DeepSearch puts Monte Carlo tree search inside RLVR training (global frontier selection, entropy-guided negatives, a replay buffer, Tree-GRPO) on a 1.5B math model; the headline gain is about one point over the previous 1.5B result.
- [[Demystifying Agentic RL (DemyAgent-4B)]] — An empirical study of tool-integrated RL on a code interpreter: real end-to-end SFT trajectories, a GRPO-TCR recipe (token-level loss, higher clip bound, overlong shaping), diverse model-aware data and entropy balance; the 4B result reports matching larger agents on AIME (self-reported).
- [[DIET Architecture]] — Dual Intent and Entity Transformer (Rasa, 2020) — a multi-task NLU architecture for joint intent classification and entity recognition that outperforms fine-tuned BERT while being 6x faster to train, using plug-and-play pre-trained embeddings with sparse features.
- [[Distilling Step-by-Step]] — Distilling step-by-step extracts rationales from a large LLM with few-shot chain-of-thought and trains a small T5 multi-task to predict both label and rationale, so it needs less labelled data and a far smaller model than the LLM (self-reported).
- [[DoRA Weight-Decomposed Low-Rank Adaptation]] — DoRA splits a pretrained weight into a magnitude vector and a direction, trains the magnitude directly and the direction with LoRA, and merges back with no inference overhead; commonsense-8 average on LLaMA-7B 74.7 to 78.4 over LoRA (self-reported).
- [[DPO Loss and Implicit Reward]] — Direct Preference Optimization rewrites the Bradley-Terry preference loss over the policy itself, using beta times the log-ratio to a reference model as an implicit reward, and what the DPO paper measured against PPO RLHF at up to 6B parameters.
- [[Dr. MAMR Lazy-Agent Multi-Agent RL]] — Dr. MAMR traces the collapse of trained two-agent reasoning pairs into one working agent to a turn-count normalization in multi-turn GRPO, and fixes it with a debiased loss, a causal-influence reward and a restart reward, scoring above single-agent GRPO on seven math benchmarks (self-reported).
- [[Dr. Zero Self-Evolving Search Agents]] — Dr. Zero trains a proposer and a solver search agent from one base model with no human questions, rewarding the proposer for questions the solver gets right only sometimes and normalising its advantage per hop count (HRPO); at 3B it roughly matched supervised Search-R1 on average (self-reported).
- [[DreamerV3 World Model with Fixed Hyperparameters]] — DreamerV3 learns a world model from replayed experience and trains an actor and critic only on imagined latent trajectories, using symlog, twohot, free-bits KL and return-normalisation tricks so one hyperparameter table runs across 8 benchmarks, including Minecraft diamonds from scratch (self-reported).
- [[DreamGym Synthetic Experience RL]] — DreamGym replaces the real environment with an LLM experience model that predicts next states and rewards, plus a variance-based task curriculum; agents trained only on its output reached roughly the level of RL on 80K real transitions in two of three environments (self-reported).
- [[DualDistill Agentic-R1]] — Distil two teachers, one that reasons in text and one that solves with Python code, into a single student by composing trajectories where one teacher's failure is followed by the other's correction; the 7B student chooses between code and text per problem on math.
- [[Dyna Planning]] — Dyna runs direct RL, model learning and planning side by side: after each real step it replays n simulated one-step updates from a learned model, which cut the episodes needed on a maze from about 25 to 3; Dyna-Q+ and prioritized sweeping handle changed environments and update focus.
- [[Eligibility Traces and TD(lambda)]] — An eligibility trace is a decaying memory vector that lets a TD error update recently visited states, so TD(lambda) interpolates between one-step TD (lambda=0) and Monte Carlo (lambda=1); the same trace ideas are mapped by Sutton and Barto onto stimulus traces in animal learning and onto dopamine-driven learning.
- [[Evol-Instruct (WizardLM and WizardCoder)]] — Evol-Instruct has an LLM rewrite existing instructions into harder or rarer ones over several rounds and fine-tunes on the merged result; WizardLM applies it to Alpaca's seeds and WizardCoder to Code Alpaca, with the authors attributing gains to instruction complexity (self-reported, preliminary).
- [[Fast-Slow Thinking Architecture]] — Three ways to give a real-time agent deep reasoning without blocking it — fast fillers then slow answers, a fast interaction model advised by a background reasoner (the 2026 mainstream), or one model that thinks while speaking — plus the text-vs-latent channel between them and the rule that the fast side never claims 'done' before the slow side confirms.
- [[Flan-PaLM Instruction Finetuning Scaling]] — Chung et al. (2022) instruction-finetune T5 and PaLM on up to 1,836 tasks including nine chain-of-thought datasets, and report how held-out gains scale with task count and model size, at about 0.2% of pretraining compute for Flan-PaLM 540B (self-reported).
- [[Focused Transformer Crossbatch Training]] — Focused Transformer trains memory attention with keys from other documents as negatives (crossbatch training) to counter the distraction issue; LongLLaMA 3B reached 94.5% passkey accuracy at 100K tokens (self-reported).
- [[Foundation Models as Decision Makers (MDP View)]] — A 2023 survey frames dialogue, prompting and tool use by a foundation model as sequential decision problems in a Markov decision process, with the model as policy and tools, humans or the world as environment; it reports no experiments, only framing and cited numbers.
- [[FrogNano Online Task Synthesis]] — Train a 4B coding agent with RL on synthetic tasks regenerated each iteration to match the current policy's solve rate (TaskPilot); five iterations of 300 tasks took SWE-bench Verified from 39.4% to 61.5% with no distillation of trajectories.
- [[GDPO Decoupled Reward Normalization]] — GDPO normalises each reward separately within the rollout group, sums the per-reward advantages, then normalises over the batch, because summing rewards before GRPO's group normalisation collapses distinct reward combinations into identical advantages.
- [[Generalized Advantage Estimation]] — GAE(gamma, lambda) estimates the advantage as an exponentially weighted sum of one-step TD residuals, with lambda trading bias against variance between the one-step TD advantage and the full Monte Carlo return minus a baseline.
- [[GRPO Group Relative Policy Optimization]] — A PPO variant with no value model: sample a group of outputs per question and use group-normalised rewards as advantages, with KL added to the loss; the DeepSeekMath paper's RL algorithm, with its reported MATH and GSM8K gains and its Maj@K versus Pass@K finding.
- [[GSPO Group Sequence Policy Optimization]] — GRPO with the importance ratio and clipping moved from tokens to whole responses, proposed to stabilise RL on large mixture-of-experts models without Routing Replay; the evidence is training curves on one Qwen3-30B-A3B-Base run.
- [[GUI Agent RL with GUI-R1 and ARPO]] — Two GRPO recipes for GUI agents: GUI-R1 uses rule-based rewards on 3K filtered examples for action prediction, while ARPO adds a replay buffer of successful trajectories so groups of all failures still carry signal; both report gains with large caveats in their own text.
- [[Harmful Fine-Tuning of Aligned Models]] — Qi et al. find that fine-tuning an aligned model on 10 to 100 harmful or identity-shifting examples, or even on benign data, raises its harmfulness rate (GPT-3.5 Turbo from 1.8% to 91.8% at 100 shots), and test mixed-in safety data as a mitigation (self-reported).
- [[Harness-Zero Harness Distillation]] — Harness-Zero evolves a task harness, uses a reviewer agent to correct the student's own proposals during data collection, and SFTs the corrections into the weights so the harness can be removed at deployment; reported macro-average success exceeds the base model with the harness attached (self-reported).
- [[Hierarchical Reinforcement Learning]] — Hierarchical RL splits a policy into low-level skills and a high-level planner; a textbook survey covers HIRO, DIAYN and HAAR, and when hierarchy is worth it, alongside the MDP variants (POMDP, contextual MDP) that precede it.
- [[Hierarchical Story Generation]] — Generate a short premise first, then condition a convolutional seq2seq story model on it, using gated multi-scale decoder self-attention and fusion with a pretrained model; introduces the WritingPrompts dataset, with stories decoded by top-k sampling (k=10).
- [[Imitation Learning and Inverse RL]] — Using expert demonstrations in RL: behavior cloning and why it is unpopular, DQfD, GAIL and SQIL, inverse RL that learns a reward from demonstrations, and the related curriculum, residual and transfer ideas, as surveyed in an industrial-RL textbook.
- [[Impossible Distillation]] — Impossible Distillation samples sentence pairs from a small GPT-2-scale model, filters them with off-the-shelf critics, trains T5-large, then self-distils; the 770M student beats same-size models trained on larger or ChatGPT-made corpora (self-reported).
- [[Interpreter-Verified Self-Generated Programming Puzzles]] — A code LM proposes programming puzzles and solutions, a Python interpreter keeps only the verified pairs, and fine-tuning on them improves the LM on held-out puzzles; GPT-Neo-2.7B rose from 7.5% to 20.6% Pass@100 on its own data and 38.2% on Codex's (self-reported).
- [[KARL Multi-Task RL Knowledge Agents]] — KARL trains a search agent with iterative large-batch off-policy RL (OAPL) on two tasks, using agent-synthesised questions and a learned compression tool; the result matched a frontier model on a six-task suite at lower cost (self-reported) and generalised better than SFT distillation.
- [[Kimi-Dev Agentless Skill Prior]] — Kimi-Dev trains a 72B model on single-turn Agentless SWE tasks (mid-training, SFT, RL) and then adapts it to a multi-turn SWE-Agent with a few thousand trajectories; the RL-stage prior matched the base prior's best score with far fewer agent tokens (self-reported).
- [[Knowledge Distillation with Soft Targets]] — Hinton et al. (2015) train a small model on a large model's temperature-softened class probabilities mixed with the hard labels, transferring most of an ensemble's gain; the soft targets also act as a regulariser.
- [[KnowNo Conformal Ask-for-Help]] — KnowNo has an LLM planner propose multiple-choice options, turns next-token probabilities into conformal prediction scores, and asks a human when the set is not a single option; with 400 calibration examples it gives a task-success guarantee, and cuts help by 10-24% in simulation (self-reported).
- [[LaMer Meta-RL Exploration]] — LaMer trains a language agent on trials of several sequential episodes with a reflection written after each failure, optimising a cross-episode return; pass@3 beat GiGPO on Sokoban, MineSweeper and Webshop (self-reported) while first-attempt success was mixed.
- [[LIMA: Small Curated Instruction Data]] — LIMA fine-tunes a 65B LLaMa on 1,000 hand-curated prompt-response pairs with supervised loss only, tests the Superficial Alignment Hypothesis, and reports responses equal or preferred to GPT-4 in 43% of human comparisons; ablations favour data diversity and quality over quantity (self-reported).
- [[LLaMA-Adapter Zero-Initialized Attention]] — LLaMA-Adapter prefixes learnable prompts to the top layers of a frozen LLaMA and scales their attention by a gate that starts at zero, so training does not disturb the model early; 1.2M trainable parameters (self-reported).
- [[LLM Prompting for Mobile UI Screens]] — A 2022 paper converts an Android view hierarchy to HTML and prompts PaLM with at most two exemplars for four screen-language tasks; one example in the prompt delivers most of the improvement, and results are rater-study and benchmark numbers with no agent loop (self-reported).
- [[LongMem Side-Network Memory]] — LongMem caches key-value pairs from a frozen backbone layer into a 65k FIFO memory bank and reads them through a trainable SideNet with gated joint attention; it reached 40.5% on ChapterBreak AO3 at an 8k prefix (self-reported).
- [[LoReFT Representation Finetuning]] — ReFT trains small interventions on a frozen model's hidden states instead of weight updates; LoReFT edits the residual stream inside a rank-r subspace and uses 15x to 65x fewer parameters than LoRA (self-reported), but is weak on arithmetic reasoning.
- [[MemLong Retrieval Memory Layers]] — MemLong caches chunk key-value pairs from a frozen lower-layer stack and retrieves them with an external dense retriever into upper layers; OpenLLaMA-3B kept lowering perplexity past 2048 tokens (self-reported).
- [[MIMESIS User Simulator Training]] — MIMESIS trains a 9B user simulator to behave like real users and uses it as the training environment for interactive agents; an agent trained against it scored higher on nine held-out simulators than one trained against GPT-5.5 (self-reported), and coached self-distillation added a further gain.
- [[MoE-LLaVA and MoE-Tuning]] — MoE-LLaVA turns a vision-language model sparse in three stages, copying the feed-forward layer into experts in the last stage; a 3.6B-activated model beats LLaVA-1.5-7B on some benchmarks and POPE against 13B (self-reported).
- [[Multi-Agent Evolve Self-Play]] — Multi-Agent Evolve trains one model in three roles (proposer, solver, judge) with reinforcement learning on unlabelled seed questions; quality filtering and partial reference use were the stabilisers, on a single 3B model.
- [[Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning]] — MARL, the non-stationarity problem it exists to solve, CTDE as the dominant answer, the value-decomposition and central-critic algorithm families, and the five challenges — including the ~10–20 agent scalability wall.
- [[Multitask Prompted Instruction Tuning (FLAN, T0, Super-NaturalInstructions, MetaICL)]] — Four 2021-2022 papers fine-tune a language model on many tasks rewritten as instructions or prompts and test it on held-out tasks: FLAN, T0, Super-NaturalInstructions (Tk-Instruct) and MetaICL. Each paper's result, with its setup, is below, along with where they disagree.
- [[N-Step Bootstrapping]] — An n-step return sums n real rewards and then bootstraps from the value estimate n steps later, spanning one-step TD (n = 1) to Monte Carlo (n = episode length); the chapter extends it to Sarsa, off-policy importance sampling with control variates, tree backup and Q(sigma).
- [[Neural Probabilistic Language Model]] — Bengio et al. (2003) introduced the idea of learning distributed word representations (embeddings) jointly with a neural network language model — fighting the curse of dimensionality by mapping words to a continuous vector space where semantically similar words have nearby representations.
- [[On-Policy Distillation]] — The student generates its own trajectories and a teacher scores every token by its full next-token distribution (minimise KL) — RL's on-policy data with SFT's dense signal, matching RL on math in about a tenth of the steps; self-distillation (OPSD) gets the same signal from the model itself given privileged context.
- [[OpenAssistant OASST1 Conversation Trees]] — A crowd-sourced, human-written and human-ranked assistant conversation corpus built by growing conversation trees one task at a time, with the SFT, reward and RLHF models trained on it and the authors' hypothesis for why RLHF gains were small.
- [[OpenCUA and AgentNet]] — OpenCUA is an open recipe for desktop computer-use agents built on AgentNet, a 22,625-task human-annotated trajectory set, with reflective long chain-of-thought synthesized per step; the 72B model reaches 45.0% on OSWorld-Verified at 100 steps (self-reported).
- [[Operational Reinforcement Learning]] — Taking an RL prototype into production, per an industrial-RL textbook: scaling architectures, how many seeds an evaluation needs, canary and shadow rollout, logging, and the safe, secure and ethical RL checklists.
- [[OSS-Instruct (Magicoder)]] — OSS-Instruct shows a teacher LLM a random open-source code snippet and has it write a self-contained problem and solution, aiming for more diverse and less biased code instruction data than Self-Instruct or Evol-Instruct; Magicoder models trained on about 75K such pairs are reported to beat the base models on HumanEval+ (self-reported).
- [[Outcome vs Process Reward Models]] — An outcome reward model scores the whole rollout and a process reward model scores each reasoning step. ORM is cheap but cannot tell a lucky guess from sound reasoning. PRM assigns credit better but needs step-level data, and in 2025 practice swung back to ORM with a light PRM used only for verification.
- [[PAPO Process Rewards for Diffusion LLMs]] — PAPO gives each denoising step of a diffusion LLM a reward (the task reward of the one-step denoised completion) and updates the policy on states re-enacted from the real rollout, applied as GRPO-style RL to LLaDA-8B-Instruct.
- [[Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning Methods]] — The PEFT family compared: freeze the base model and train a small piece (adapters, a low-rank update, a prefix or prompt, bias terms, a representation intervention), plus the memory arithmetic and hyperparameter advice from Huyen's finetuning chapter.
- [[Past-Tense Refusal Jailbreak]] — Rewriting a harmful request in the past tense bypasses refusal training on several frontier models, and fine-tuning on past-tense refusals closes the gap at a cost in overrefusal.
- [[PDDL-INSTRUCT Logical-CoT Planning]] — PDDL-INSTRUCT tunes an LLM to write symbolic plans step by step, restating preconditions, effects and the full state after every action, with an external plan validator supplying feedback during training; Llama-3-8B reaches 94% valid plans on Blocksworld (self-reported, three PlanBench domains).
- [[Phi-4 Training Recipe and Pivotal Token Search]] — Phi-4 is a 14B model trained on a synthetic-heavy mixture, then post-trained with SFT and two DPO rounds, the first built from Pivotal Token Search pairs; it beats GPT-4o on GPQA and MATH (self-reported) but is weak at strict instruction following.
- [[PivotRL Pivot-Turn Agentic RL]] — PivotRL profiles turns of expert trajectories, keeps only the turns where sampled actions disagree, and runs short local RL with a verifier that accepts any acceptable action; it beat same-data SFT on three of four agentic benchmarks and kept out-of-domain scores near base (self-reported).
- [[Position Interpolation]] — Position Interpolation (PI) extends a RoPE model's context window by scaling position indices down into the trained range instead of extrapolating past it, and recovers a working 8K to 32K window from LLaMA after about 1,000 fine-tuning steps (self-reported).
- [[Post-Training for Agents]] — How SFT, RL and distillation divide the work of training an agent — SFT writes form and protocol, RL learns transferable strategy, on-policy distillation gives RL's on-policy data with SFT's dense signal — and why the environment, data and reward shape matter more than the choice of algorithm.
- [[Post-Training Reasoning Data Primer]] — A 2026 survey of over 150 public studies on the data side of reasoning post-training: difficulty is relative to the base model, small curated sets work only inside the base model's support, coverage is a recipe, and self-play moves curation rather than removing it; it reports no results of its own.
- [[Preference Optimization Algorithms]] — The PPO → DPO → GRPO → KTO/IPO/ORPO family — what each removes from the stage before it, the five-algorithm decision table, and why the field shifted from choosing an algorithm to designing a reward structure.
- [[Prefix-Tuning and Prompt Tuning]] — Two ways to train continuous vectors in front of a frozen language model: prefix-tuning (activations at every layer, for generation) and prompt tuning (embeddings at the input only, whose gap to full tuning closes as the model grows).
- [[Q-Learning]] — Watkins's 1989 thesis defines Q-learning as incremental value iteration over action values and proves one-step tabular Q-learning converges via a notional action-replay process; its multi-step form and the meta-stable policy problem are left without proof.
- [[QLoRA 4-Bit Quantized Finetuning]] — QLoRA trains LoRA adapters through a frozen 4-bit NF4 base model, with double quantization of the constants and paged optimizers, bringing 65B finetuning under 48 GB (self-reported) while matching 16-bit LoRA on the tested setups.
- [[Real-Time Multimodal Agents]] — Voice dialogue, computer use and robot control are the three settings where continuous input and a strict output time budget make multimodality hard — and all three are moving the same way, from serial pipelines toward end-to-end models and a fast/slow division of labour, with irreversible actions needing a gate.
- [[Recurrent Memory Transformer (RMT)]] — RMT passes a few memory tokens from one fixed-size segment to the next on a pretrained transformer, giving linear compute and constant memory; it reached 2M tokens on synthetic fact tasks, though memory is lossy and the tasks are easy.
- [[Reflection in Pre-Training]] — A study of OLMo-2 checkpoints reports that self-correction behaviour already appears during plain pre-training and grows with pre-training compute, measured by injecting errors into chains of thought and appending 'Wait,'; it argues against reflection being a product of RL alone.
- [[REINFORCE with a Baseline]] — REINFORCE (Williams 1992) updates each weight by a rate times an offset reinforcement times the characteristic eligibility, so the expected update follows the gradient of expected reward; any baseline independent of the current output leaves that expectation unchanged, and the gradient bandit is the same idea.
- [[Reinforcement Learning Foundations]] — The MDP tuple (S, A, P, R, γ), the Markov property that makes RL tractable, the four algorithm families, and the exploration/exploitation tension — with how each maps onto LLM training and agentic tool use.
- [[Reinforcement-Learned Teachers]] — Train a small model with RL to write explanations for known question-solution pairs, rewarded by how well a student can reproduce the solution from them; in the paper a 7B teacher's raw traces gave better student models than traces distilled from much larger reasoners.
- [[Rejection Sampling Fine-Tuning]] — Sample several candidates per prompt, keep only those a rule, test or reference answer verifies, deduplicate and cap per prompt, SFT on the survivors, and repeat as the model improves — the STaR/RFT loop that sits between SFT and RL with no reward model and no policy gradient.
- [[ReST Meets ReAct Agent Self-Improvement]] — Recipe that runs a prompted multi-step search agent, ranks its trajectories with an LLM, and fine-tunes on them for two iterations using AI feedback only; reported to bring PaLM 2 XS and S close to the prompted L teacher (self-reported).
- [[Reward Hacking and Overoptimization]] — The three failure modes of optimizing against a learned reward proxy — reward hacking, the inverse-U overoptimization curve against KL distance, and the ~15% alignment tax on academic NLP benchmarks.
- [[Reward Model Overoptimization Scaling Laws]] — Gao, Schulman and Hilton fit functional forms for gold reward against sqrt(KL) for best-of-n and RL against proxy reward models in a synthetic gold-RM setup, and report how the coefficients move with RM size, RM data, policy size and KL penalty.
- [[RISE Recursive Introspection]] — Fine-tune one model on multi-turn rollouts where it sees its earlier wrong answers and revises them, so that sequential revision beats parallel sampling on math; in the paper, prompted self-refine lowered accuracy and plain single-turn SFT on the same data did not teach it.
- [[RL Reward and State Engineering]] — Practitioner guidance from an industrial-RL textbook on spotting an RL problem, then engineering the environment, state, actions, exploration and reward, including its reward-clipping and discretisation cautions.
- [[RL Training Frameworks for LLMs]] — verl, OpenRLHF, and TRL are the three open-source backbones for RL on language models: verl for 70B+ reasoning runs, OpenRLHF for the broadest production use up to ~30B, and TRL for the flattest learning curve up to ~13B. Pick by model scale and algorithm, not popularity.
- [[RLHF Pipeline]] — The three-stage InstructGPT pipeline — SFT, then a Bradley-Terry reward model on ~33k comparisons, then PPO with a KL penalty against the SFT policy — and why all three stages are load-bearing.
- [[RLMF Metacognitive Advantage Scaling]] — Reinforcement learning with metacognitive feedback scales each GRPO completion's advantage by how accurately the model judged its own confidence faithfulness, instead of adding a reward term; on faithful calibration of small models it beat plain GRPO in the paper's runs.
- [[RLOO REINFORCE Leave-One-Out]] — RLOO samples k completions per prompt and uses the mean reward of the other k-1 as each sample's baseline, treating the whole generation as one action; one paper reports it beating PPO, DPO and RAFT on TL;DR and Anthropic-HH with 6.9B and 7B models.
- [[RLVP — Verifiable Path Penalties]] — Add deterministic per-action penalties (and matching compliant-action bonuses) to an outcome reward, R = O + β·Φ, so agent RL learns outcome-neutral constraints like 'don't edit the tests' and recovers the within-group variance GRPO needs — with four design rules that avoid the inaction trap.
- [[RLVR Pass@k Reasoning Boundary]] — Yue et al.'s pass@k study of whether RL with verifiable rewards gives a model reasoning ability beyond its base model: at large k the base model catches up with and overtakes the RL model, and distillation behaves differently.
- [[RoboCat Self-Improving Robot Generalist]] — RoboCat trains one vision-based goal-conditioned policy across robot arms and tasks, fine-tunes it on a few hundred demonstrations, lets it collect its own data and retrains; the appendix evidence shows self-generated data is mostly failures and matches the data's success rate (self-reported).
- [[Role Anchor and Role Drift]] — Role drift is the loss of a module's assigned job when a compound LLM pipeline is trained end to end with a final-answer reward; the Role Anchor penalty on a role-utility measure kept a RAG reader evidence-following (0.869 against 0.589) in a small study (self-reported).
- [[rStar2-Agent GRPO-RoC]] — Resample-on-Correct keeps a binary answer-only reward for tool-integrated math RL and cleans up trajectory quality by choosing which rollouts to train on; a 14B model trained this way reports frontier-level AIME pass@1 in 510 steps (self-reported).
- [[RULER and ART Hybrid Reward Agent Training]] — RULER uses an LLM judge to rank an agent's trajectories relative to one another, and ART trains on those scores with GRPO-style group-normalised rewards; for verifiable tasks the chapter gates a RULER quality term behind a binary correctness check.
- [[RuleTaker]] — Clark et al. fine-tune RoBERTa-large on synthetic natural-language facts and rules and report it answers true/false questions at about 99% on its own test data, generalises to deeper proofs, and transfers to paraphrased rules, while stopping short of proving it reasons (self-reported).
- [[s1 Budget Forcing]] — s1: SFT of Qwen2.5-32B-Instruct on 1,000 selected reasoning traces plus budget forcing, a decoding-time cap or extension of thinking length by appending or suppressing the end-of-thinking delimiter; with LIMO's 800-example result as a comparison.
- [[Salesforce Koa]] — Koa is Salesforce's Nemotron-3-Super-120B model trained with a GRPO variant in spec-driven CRM environments; it scored 87% on the internal CRMAgentBench against 82% for GPT-4.1, while giving back 2.3 BFCL points after the in-domain stage (all self-reported, single runs).
- [[SayCan Affordance-Grounded Skill Selection]] — SayCan picks a robot's next skill by multiplying a language model's score for the skill text by a learned value-function affordance for the current scene; with PaLM 540B on 101 instructions it planned correctly 84% of the time and executed 74% in a mock kitchen (self-reported).
- [[Scheduled Sampling]] — A training schedule for recurrent sequence generators that mixes the true previous token and the model's own sampled token as decoder input, with the true-token probability decaying over training, to narrow the gap between training and decoding.
- [[SDPO Self-Distillation Policy Optimization]] — SDPO re-scores each rollout under the same model reprompted with environment feedback and distils that self-teacher's next-token predictions into the policy, giving token-level credit where GRPO gives one scalar; tested on Qwen3-8B and Olmo3-7B-Instruct.
- [[Search-R1 Reasoning with Search RL]] — Search-R1 trains an LLM with outcome-only reinforcement learning to interleave reasoning with live search calls, masking retrieved text out of the loss; on seven QA sets it beat RAG and prompting baselines, and removing the mask cost 0.088 average exact match at 7B (self-reported).
- [[Self-Instruct and Unnatural Instructions]] — Two 2022 methods that prompt a language model to write its own instruction-tuning data from a few human seeds: Self-Instruct bootstraps 52K instructions from 175 seed tasks, Unnatural Instructions generates 240,670 examples from 15 seeds; both report noisy data and benchmark results near human-curated sets (self-reported).
- [[Self-Play SWE-RL (SSR)]] — One policy plays bug injector and bug solver on bare Docker images, with bugs specified only by a test-weakening patch, trained jointly with RL; the paper reports gains on SWE-bench Verified and SWE-Bench Pro over a human-data RL baseline (self-reported).
- [[Self-Rewarding Language Models]] — One model both answers and judges: LLM-as-a-Judge scores on its own samples build the DPO pairs for each next iteration; on Llama 2 70B three iterations improved instruction following and judging, with length growth uncontrolled.
- [[SEM First-Answer-Gated Search Reward]] — SEM trains a search-using model with GRPO to answer first and search only if the first answer is wrong, by paying reward only on a correct no-search first answer or on a post-search answer after a wrong one; the paper reports a very low search ratio on questions that need none (self-reported).
- [[Semantic Turn Detection]] — Replacing VAD's silence threshold with streaming perception that judges end-of-turn from meaning plus acoustics — why VAD + segmented ASR fails on latency, information and accuracy, the two streaming paths, and the label-leakage bug where the fix was causal re-labelling, not a new architecture.
- [[Semi-Gradient TD and Tile Coding]] — With a parameterised value function, each RL update becomes a supervised example; bootstrapping targets make the update semi-gradient, linear semi-gradient TD(0) converges to the TD fixed point within 1/(1-gamma) of the best error, and tile coding with offset tilings is the book's practical feature construction.
- [[SimPO and PRO Reference-Free Preference Optimization]] — Two alternatives to DPO: SimPO scores a response by its length-normalised average log-likelihood with a target margin, PRO contrasts the best response against all lower-ranked ones over a ranking of any length, each with its own evaluation setup and limits.
- [[SkillGym]] — SkillGym is a set of 2,756 containerised task environments built around human skills, with 8,364 verified teacher trajectories used for SFT; fine-tuning a Qwen3.5-35B-A3B student lifted GDPval-AA, Terminal-Bench 2.1 and SkillsBench scores, and the trained agent without skills beat its skill-assisted base (self-reported).
- [[SkillRL Hierarchical Skill Bank]] — SkillRL has a teacher distil an agent's successes and failures into a two-level skill library that is retrieved into context while a GRPO policy trains, and extended whenever a task category falls below a success threshold; on ALFWorld it reached 89.9% against 77.6% for GRPO alone (self-reported).
- [[Soft Actor-Critic and Maximum-Entropy RL]] — Maximum-entropy RL adds an entropy term, weighted by a temperature, to the reward; a practitioner textbook covers soft actor-critic, its automatic-temperature and discrete variants, grid-world demos of the temperature, and a Donkey Car build.
- [[STaR Self-Taught Reasoner]] — Few-shot prompt a model for rationales, keep those that reach the correct answer, rationalize failures by giving the answer as a hint, fine-tune the original model on the union and repeat; GPT-J results on CommonsenseQA, arithmetic and GSM8K.
- [[Stored Instruction Computer LLM Memory Universality]] — Schuurmans shows a frozen LLM plus an external associative memory and a regex loop can run a universal Turing machine, verified by 29 greedy prompts on Flan-U-PaLM 540B; it is a computability argument, not evidence about how agents use memory.
- [[Subliminal Learning]] — A student model fine-tuned on a teacher's outputs on unrelated data can pick up the teacher's traits, including misalignment, when both start from the same initialization, and filtering the data does not reliably prevent it.
- [[Synthetic Computers at Scale]] — A pipeline that turns personas into synthetic computers with files, collaborators and a month of simulated work, then distils skills from run retrospectives; the skills lifted a held-out score from 61.6% to 68.6% (self-reported) with no weight updates.
- [[Synthetic Instruction Data: Curation and Verification]] — Huyen's dataset-engineering chapter frames post-training data by quality, coverage and quantity and gives a practitioner checklist for synthesising, verifying, distilling and processing it; this page records her guidance and reported figures, and points to the paper pages for each generation method.
- [[Temporal-Difference Learning, Sarsa and Expected Sarsa]] — TD learning updates a value estimate from the next step's estimate instead of a full return; Sarsa, Q-learning and Expected Sarsa differ only in the target they bootstrap from, which decides whether the learned policy accounts for its own exploration.
- [[The Deadly Triad]] — Instability and divergence arise whenever function approximation, bootstrapping and off-policy training are combined; Sutton and Barto show it with counterexamples (the w/2w example, Baird's counterexample) and present Gradient-TD and Emphatic-TD as remedies.
- [[Tool-N1 Binary-Reward Tool-Calling RL]] — Tool-N1 trains Qwen2.5 models for tool calling with GRPO and a binary reward on output format plus exact call match, with no reasoning-trace supervision; its 7B and 14B models edge past GPT-4o on single-turn BFCL (self-reported), and pure RL matches SFT-then-RL by a hair.
- [[Toolformer Self-Supervised Tool Use]] — Fine-tuning recipe in which a model samples API calls into plain text, executes them and keeps only those that lower the loss on following tokens; GPT-J 6.7B so trained is reported to beat GPT-3 175B on several zero-shot tasks (self-reported).
- [[Training-Agent Disaggregation]] — Make an existing agent trainable without rewriting it: keep the runtime as-is, trace each run as state → action → reward into an external trainer, bind trainable roles to logical model names a proxy can re-route to a learning policy, and train each role with the algorithm that fits it (SFT, PPO, contextual bandit).
- [[Transformer Architecture]] — The Transformer model architecture (Vaswani et al., 2017) — encoder-decoder stacks of self-attention and feed-forward layers that replaced RNNs/CNNs for sequence transduction, enabling parallelized training and constant-length dependency paths.
- [[Trust Region and Clipped Surrogate Policy Optimization]] — TRPO limits each policy update with a KL trust region solved by conjugate gradient and a line search; PPO replaces that machinery with a clipped probability-ratio objective that allows several minibatch epochs per batch. Both keep the new policy close to the one that collected the data.
- [[Tulu 3 Post-Training Recipe and RLVR]] — Tulu 3's four-stage open post-training pipeline on Llama 3.1 (decontaminated prompts, SFT, length-normalised DPO on on-policy preference data, then PPO with a binary verifiable reward called RLVR) and the ablations it reports for each stage.
- [[UCRL2 Average-Reward Regret]] — UCRL2 learns an unknown finite MDP optimistically: it plans in an extended MDP built from L1 confidence balls and replans only when a state-action count doubles, giving regret of order D S sqrt(A n log n) against sqrt(D S A n) as the lower bound.
- [[UI-TARS Native GUI Agent]] — UI-TARS trains one vision-language model on screenshots to emit thoughts and keyboard-mouse actions, replacing prompted agent frameworks; the 72B DPO model reports 24.6 on OSWorld at 50 steps against 22.0 for Claude Computer Use (self-reported).
- [[UltraChat and LaMini Instruction Datasets]] — Two 2023 scale-oriented ChatGPT-generated instruction sets: UltraChat (1.5M multi-turn dialogues built by simulating the user, training UltraLM-13B) and LaMini (2.58M single-turn pairs distilled into 61M to 7B models); both papers report results that depend on GPT-4 or small human evaluations (self-reported).
- [[UltraFeedback Scaled AI Feedback Dataset]] — A GPT-4-annotated preference and critique dataset over 63,967 instructions and 17 models, built with aspect decomposition and joint scoring of four completions, plus the UltraRM reward model and UltraCM critique model trained on it.
- [[Unlimiformer]] — Unlimiformer replaces cross-attention in a pretrained encoder-decoder with a kNN lookup over the encoder states of the whole input, so one index serves every head and layer; it improves summarization at book length, and does nothing when the input is already ranked.
- [[World Knowledge Model for Agent Planning]] — WKM gives an agent a separately trained knowledge model: task knowledge written once from contrasting good and bad trajectories, and state knowledge retrieved per step and mixed into action probabilities; 7-8B models beat fine-tuned baselines on ALFWorld, WebShop and ScienceWorld (self-reported).
- [[XP3, BLOOMZ and mT0]] — Muennighoff et al. (2023) finetune BLOOM and mT5 on xP3, a 46-language and code prompted mixture, and find zero-shot gains on tasks and languages not intentionally seen, but no HumanEval gain at 176B and a drop on English prompts when machine-translated prompts are added (self-reported).

## Frameworks

- [[ADK Context Engineering]] — How the ADK samples repo manages context — SKILL.md pattern, three skill-loading strategies, static vs dynamic instruction, and history compaction.
- [[ADK Deployment Patterns]] — ADK deployment targets (Agent Engine vs Cloud Run vs GKE), CI/CD with WIF, service account architecture, event-driven triggers, and Terraform patterns.
- [[ADK Eval Guide]] — ADK evaluation methodology — the eval-fix loop, 8 built-in criteria, evalset schema, tool trajectory gotchas, multimodal eval, user simulation, and Vertex AI managed pointwise/pairwise eval.
- [[ADK JS TypeScript Patterns]] — Google ADK TypeScript SDK (@google/adk 0.5.0) — LlmAgent, FunctionTool, structured Zod output, streaming NDJSON, and pitfall patterns for Next.js agent integration.
- [[ADK Observability]] — Four-tier observability for ADK agents — Cloud Trace (always-on), prompt-response logging, BigQuery Agent Analytics plugin, and third-party platforms (AgentOps, Phoenix, MLflow, etc.).
- [[ADK Python API Reference]] — Quick reference for the Google ADK Python SDK — agent types, tools, state, callbacks, plugins, artifacts, memory, context caching, and context compaction.
- [[ADK Scaffold Patterns]] — Agent Starter Pack CLI patterns for scaffolding ADK agent projects — templates, deployment options, prototype-first workflow, DESIGN_SPEC.md contract, and development phase guidelines.
- [[ADK User Simulation Eval]] — Dynamic conversation testing in ADK using ConversationScenario and a user simulator LLM — replaces static turn sequences when agent response order is unpredictable.
- [[ADK vs LangGraph Comparison]] — Side-by-side mental model comparison of Google ADK and LangGraph — primitive mappings, weighted scoring (LangGraph 716/845 for AWS/ADK-compatible context), assistant-team production findings, and the recommended vocabulary alignment approach.
- [[ADK vs LangGraph Decision]] — Decision to keep Librarian on LangGraph — ADK's strengths don't address Librarian's core requirements; vocabulary alignment (Level 1) is the right refactor scope.
- [[ADK Workflow Agents]] — ADK's three deterministic workflow agents — Sequential, Parallel, and Loop — which provide control flow without LLM orchestration.
- [[Context Graph Is Not an Execution Graph]] — Decision to keep a recommendation product's intelligence layer as plain TypeScript in one repo — a relationship graph is a data asset, not an orchestration runtime, and LangGraph/ADK are adopted only for the specific workflow that needs stateful orchestration, behind a named trigger.
- [[Deep Agents Framework]] — Opinionated agent harness built on LangChain/LangGraph — create_deep_agent() wraps planning, file management, subagent delegation, and HITL into configurable middleware with no boilerplate.
- [[Deep Agents Memory Backends]] — Pluggable backend system for Deep Agents file operations and memory — StateBackend (ephemeral), StoreBackend (cross-thread), FilesystemBackend (local disk), and CompositeBackend (routing).
- [[Dialogue Transformers — TED Policy]] — Transformer Embedding Dialogue (TED) policy (Rasa, 2020) applies self-attention at the discourse level — over dialogue turns rather than tokens — outperforming LSTM-based policies on sub-dialogue handling while being simpler and faster than REDP.
- [[Framework Selection — LangChain vs LangGraph vs Deep Agents]] — Decision guide for choosing between LangChain, LangGraph, and Deep Agents — layered frameworks where higher layers add planning, memory, and middleware on top of lower ones.
- [[HistoryCondenser]] — LangGraph node that rewrites the latest user query into a self-contained form given prior turns — prevents retrieval degradation on coreference-heavy multi-turn conversations.
- [[HITL and Interrupt Patterns]] — Six HITL patterns for LangGraph agents — static breakpoints, dynamic interrupt(), clarification loop (budget-bounded), scheduler confirmation gate, tool approval for irreversible actions, and time-travel/replay/fork.
- [[LangChain Agent Middleware]] — LangChain's create_agent() middleware API — HumanInTheLoopMiddleware for tool-call approval, wrap_tool_call/before_model/after_model hooks for custom logic, and Command-based resume — the mechanism Framework Selection means when it says "LangGraph has no middleware."
- [[LangChain Dependency Management]] — Package structure and version policy for the LangChain ecosystem — langchain/langchain-core/langgraph/langsmith as the required core, provider/tool packages installed a la carte, and the langchain-community non-semver trap.
- [[LangChain Fundamentals — create_agent, Tools, Structured Output]] — LangChain 1.0's core agent-building primitives — the create_agent() loop, the @tool decorator, checkpointer-based persistence, and structured output — a framework that runs on the LangGraph runtime, over integration packages that sit beneath both.
- [[LangChain RAG Implementation Patterns]] — LangChain-specific RAG implementation surface — document loaders, RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter, vector store classes (Chroma/FAISS/Pinecone), similarity/MMR search, metadata filtering, and wrapping a retriever as an agent tool; the API layer beneath the conceptual choices in RAG Retrieval Strategies.
- [[LangGraph Advanced Patterns]] — Advanced LangGraph patterns beyond the basics — subgraphs, Send API fan-out, streaming modes, time-travel, breakpoints/interrupts, error handling, and Plan-and-Execute.
- [[LangGraph BaseStore]] — LangGraph's cross-thread persistent key-value store with optional vector search — the standard backend for episodic, semantic, and procedural agent memory.
- [[LangGraph CRAG Pipeline]] — The Corrective RAG pattern implemented as a LangGraph StateGraph — deterministic graph with conditional retry loop, confidence gating, and typed state schema.
- [[LangGraph State Reducers]] — Functions that define how parallel node outputs merge into shared state — preventing collisions when multiple nodes write to the same field simultaneously.
- [[Multi-Agent Orchestration Patterns]] — Multi-agent architecture patterns — supervisor/handoff/parallel swarm trade-offs, try-agent history for fallback routing, tool count budget, and the [client] ADK POC selection rationale.
- [[Multi-Modal Agent Response]] — Agent response pattern where output can include text, data visualizations, interactive UI components, and full task surfaces — moving beyond chat to structured elicitation and guided execution.
- [[Orchestration Architecture Decision]] — Three architecture options for the Librarian service deployment — full Bedrock, full LangGraph, or polyglot — with tradeoffs and the recommended migration path.
- [[Plan and Execute Pattern]] — Separating planning from execution for multi-step agent tasks — Planner, Executor, Replanner, and Responder nodes with HITL confirmation gate.
- [[Product Design Patterns]] — Product design patterns for embedded assistant agents — three interaction levels, structured output as UI contract, page context awareness, escalation triggers, and tool count budget for routing quality.
- [[Realtime Voice Architecture Reference]] — Numbers and tradeoffs for building a realtime voice agent — cascaded ASR→LLM→TTS vs speech-to-speech, the latency budget in which LLM time-to-first-token dominates, endpointing strategies, domain-specific transcription failure modes, and the regulated-domain constraints (GDPR Art. 9/17/22, health-data hosting) that shape the architecture before any code.
- [[Runtime Topology and Checkpointer Alignment]] — Critical rule — checkpointer backend must match runtime hosting model. MemorySaver fails silently in Lambda and multi-worker deployments. Covers trigger patterns, observability tool choice (LangSmith vs Langfuse/GDPR), and key production signals.
- [[Send API Fan-out]] — LangGraph's Send API enables dynamic map-reduce parallelism — fan out to N workers at runtime without knowing N at graph compile time.
- [[SKILL.md Pattern]] — ADK skill declaration format — YAML frontmatter listing tools + natural language instruction body, enabling dynamic skill loading without hardcoding capabilities into the system prompt.
- [[Summarization Node]] — A LangGraph node that compresses conversation history when it exceeds a trigger threshold — keeps context window usage bounded while preserving conversational continuity.
- [[System Design — Serverless Agent Backends]] — Interview-format system design writeup of running agent systems on serverless (Vercel Functions / Next.js API routes) — stateless invocations, session state in Postgres, streaming within platform timeouts, and the designed handoff to a stateful phase 2.
- [[Voice Agent Patterns]] — Patterns for real-time voice agents — hard latency constraints, BIDI streaming session management, ADK Strategy C preloading, LangGraph flat tool node, and mandatory history pruning.

## Prompting

- [[Adaptive Branching MCTS]] — AB-MCTS decides at each node whether to sample a new answer (wider) or refine an existing one using external feedback (deeper), via Thompson sampling; it had the best average rank in the paper, with margins often inside the error bars (self-reported).
- [[Adversarial Examples (Intriguing Properties of Neural Networks)]] — Szegedy et al. (2013) found that small, minimum-norm input perturbations found with L-BFGS flip a trained network's prediction, that the perturbed inputs transfer across models and training sets, and that training on them lowered test error in a preliminary run.
- [[Aggregation of Reasoning (AoR)]] — Replaces majority voting over sampled chains of thought with an LLM evaluation of the chains themselves, scored locally within answer buckets and globally across buckets, with dynamic sampling.
- [[Algorithm of Thoughts Plus (AoT+) State Memoization]] — AoT+ adds periodic restatement of the current state to a single-prompt search trace; on PDDL planning benchmarks its ablation credits the restatement, not the hand-written heuristics, and it matches or beats an external-verifier loop at about a third of the tokens (self-reported).
- [[Analogical Prompting]] — One prompt asks the model to recall relevant example problems (optionally with a tutorial) and then solve the target problem, replacing hand-labelled few-shot exemplars; helps larger models, hurts small ones.
- [[ATF Analysis-to-Filtration Prompting]] — ATF has the model list the irrelevant clauses in a problem, rewrite the question without them, then reason on the filtered text; on GSMIR (GSM8K plus one irrelevant sentence) it lifted chain-of-thought accuracy on GPT-3.5-Turbo from 50.2 to 74.9 (self-reported).
- [[Branch-Solve-Merge]] — A three-prompt program: the model plans parallel sub-tasks, solves each independently, then merges; on MT-Bench writing pairs with LLaMA-2-70B-chat it lifted judge-human agreement from 0.43 to 0.55 (self-reported).
- [[Buffer of Thoughts]] — Buffer of Thoughts distils each problem into key information and retrieves a stored thought-template from a growing meta-buffer, so one query instantiates a template instead of searching; it reports gains on 10 GPT-4 tasks at about 12% of multi-query cost, measured as time (self-reported).
- [[Capped Tiered Tag Assignment]] — An annotation rule for multi-label tags (moods, vibes) — the item's category defines a plausible range split into primary, secondary and situational tags, each item gets 1–3 (default 2) with at most one "stretch" tag, every tag needs concrete evidence, and under-tagging beats over-tagging because tag inflation makes filters and matching useless.
- [[Chain of Code]] — The model writes reasoning as code; a Python interpreter runs each line and an LM emulator fills in lines that fail to execute (semantic helpers), giving 84 on BIG-Bench Hard against 72 for CoT in the paper (self-reported, text-davinci-003).
- [[Chain of Draft]] — A prompt that asks for a terse draft of each reasoning step, about five words, instead of full chain-of-thought prose; on four easy tasks it kept most of the accuracy at a fraction of the tokens, but the limit is not enforced and zero-shot use degraded.
- [[Chain-of-Table]] — Table question answering where the LLM repeatedly picks one of five table operations, the operation is executed programmatically, and the transformed table is fed back, so the reasoning lives in the evolving table.
- [[Chain-of-Thought Prompting]] — Few-shot exemplars that include written-out intermediate reasoning steps; a prompting technique with scale-dependent gains and a contested account of why it works.
- [[Chain-of-Thought Unfaithfulness Under Biased Context]] — Turpin et al. add biasing features to prompts (always-A answers, a suggested answer, weak stereotype evidence) and find chain-of-thought explanations almost never mention the bias while accuracy falls by up to 36 points on GPT-3.5; the tests can show unfaithfulness but cannot prove faithfulness (self-reported).
- [[Chain-of-Verification]] — A four-step prompting method (draft, plan verification questions, answer them without the draft in view, write a revised answer) that raised Llama 65B's FActScore on biographies from 55.9 to 71.4 with the factor-and-revise variant (self-reported), at the cost of fewer facts.
- [[Coconut Continuous Latent Reasoning]] — Training method that feeds the model's last hidden state back as the next input embedding instead of decoding a token, using a multi-stage curriculum; reported to match or beat CoT on ProsQA with far fewer tokens but not on GSM8k (self-reported).
- [[Code Generation Prompting]] — Prompt shapes for code — instruction→code, comments→code, signature completion with stop sequences, schema→SQL, explain-code, parseable unit-test prompts and fill-in-the-middle — plus the over-refusal trap of generic safety system prompts; context and verification remain the biggest levers.
- [[Coding-Agent Skill Distillation (CASD)]] — CASD hands an unmodified coding agent a static corpus of agent trajectories and lets it analyse the whole corpus with code, then write a skill file used as the system prompt; the paper reports it beating GEPA on three of four agentic benchmarks at lower cost (self-reported).
- [[Complexity-Based Prompting]] — Choose the chain-of-thought exemplars with the most reasoning steps, and vote only among the longest sampled chains; helps large models on math, little on small ones.
- [[Context-Faithful Prompting]] — Framing the context as a narrator's statement and asking for the narrator's opinion, plus counterfactual demonstrations, makes a model follow supplied text over memorised facts and say it does not know when the text lacks the answer.
- [[Contrastive Chain of Thought]] — Contrastive Chain of Thought adds an invalid rationale to each few-shot demonstration, built by shuffling the objects in the valid one, and reports gains over plain CoT on three reasoning sets with one model; a toy theory paper offers partial support.
- [[Controllable Text Generation Taxonomy]] — A 2024 survey sorts control of LLM output into content (hard) and attribute (soft) tasks, and into training-stage and inference-stage methods; prompting, latent steering and decoding-time edits sit on the inference side. It ran no experiments, so it ranks nothing.
- [[CoT-Decoding]] — Elicits chain-of-thought paths without any prompt by branching on the top-k first tokens, continuing greedily, and choosing the path with the largest answer-confidence margin.
- [[Ctrl-G HMM Logical Control]] — Ctrl-G distills a hidden Markov model from the LLM, compiles a logical constraint to a DFA, and reweights next-token probabilities by the chance of satisfying the constraint; it reached full constraint success on text editing, but needs logit access and a distilled model per LLM.
- [[Decomposed Prompting (DecomP)]] — A decomposer prompt emits tagged sub-questions that a controller routes to separate handlers, which can be few-shot prompts, recursive decomposers or symbolic tools such as retrieval.
- [[DeepConf Confidence-Filtered Voting]] — Scores each sampled reasoning trace by token-level confidence, keeps or stops the low-confidence ones, and votes over the rest; in the paper, offline voting raised AIME accuracy by about 5 points, while a separate online mode saved up to 84.7% of tokens in its best case (self-reported); needs logprobs.
- [[Delethink Markovian Thinker]] — Delethink trains a reasoning model with RL in fixed-size chunks, resetting context at each boundary to the query plus the last m tokens, so thinking length no longer sets context size; a 1.5B model trained at 24K matched or beat LongCoT-RL at the same budget on math (self-reported).
- [[DERA Dialog-Enabled Resolving Agents]] — DERA has two GPT-4 prompts, a Researcher that points out problems and a Decider with final authority, converse over a draft and write accepted fixes to a shared scratchpad; on clinical summaries and care plans physicians preferred the result, while multiple-choice and open-ended medical QA showed little or no gain (self-reported).
- [[Direct-Indirect Reasoning (DIR)]] — Adds indirect reasoning prompts, contrapositive and proof by contradiction, alongside direct reasoning chains and votes over both, improving logical reasoning and mathematical proof tasks.
- [[Discriminator Bottleneck in LLM Planning]] — A study of re-ranking, iterative correction and tree search over sampled programs finds that the accuracy of the discriminator that scores candidates matters more than the planning method: advanced methods need roughly 90% discrimination accuracy to beat re-ranking, tree search is 10-20 times slower, and execution feedback lifts weak discriminators (self-reported).
- [[Diversity Seeding for Synthetic Data]] — Temperature alone does not make synthetic data diverse — inject the randomness into the prompt by sampling combinations of dimension values (TinyStories), generating hierarchically, varying target audience (phi-1), or few-shot query generation for retrievers (Promptagator); a seeded label is a free label.
- [[DSPy]] — DSPy replaces hand-written prompts with signatures and modules that an optimizer compiles against a metric and training set — bootstrapping and ordering few-shot demonstrations, searching instruction wordings, or distilling into weights; worth it only with a multi-step pipeline, a scorable metric and labelled data.
- [[EmotionPrompt]] — Appending a short emotional or confidence-pushing sentence (such as a remark about career importance) to a prompt; the 2023 paper reports gains across six models, but most of the headline comes from picking the best of 11 stimuli per task on the test set.
- [[Evaluating Prompts]] — Prompt-level evaluation — build the eval before the prompt; match test style to what changes (model, single prompt, composition); climb from a git-diffed example suite to an automated harness by solving sample sourcing and scoring independently; then A/B online with success and guardrail metrics fixed up front.
- [[Evidence-Labelled Generation with Deterministic QC]] — Label every evidence item E1…En before the model sees it, require each claim to cite labels, keep the output schema as plain strings so out-of-vocabulary values reach QC instead of being coerced, then run a pure rule set whose worst outcome is the verdict — so every value shown can be traced to the record it rests on.
- [[Few-Shot Prompting]] — Providing worked examples before the task — the most reliable lever for steering output format, tone and structure without fine-tuning, but examples teach more than intended (distribution, order, range), so use them only for what is genuinely non-obvious.
- [[Fine-Tuning as Prompt Compression]] — Fine-tuning is prompt engineering by other means — full fine-tuning teaches facts, LoRA teaches which existing behaviours and priors to use, soft prompting learns a vector-valued prompt; reach for it when the static prompt is long, stable and still not followed, then make prompts look like the fine-tuning documents.
- [[Flipped-Label In-Context Learning]] — Experiments flipping or replacing in-context labels show that overriding semantic priors and learning arbitrary input-label mappings both emerge with model scale, and that instruction tuning strengthens priors.
- [[FormatSpread]] — Meaning-preserving changes to prompt format (casing, separators, spaces, enumeration) move few-shot accuracy by up to 76 points on LLaMA-2-13B; FormatSpread estimates the min-max spread across formats with a bandit under a budget, without model weights (self-reported).
- [[Four Prompt Criteria]] — Every prompt must at once resemble training data (the Little Red Riding Hood principle), carry all and only the relevant information (Chekhov's gun), lead to a solution, and have a natural stopping point — chat tuning gives you three for free, never the second.
- [[Free Text With Deterministic Repair Over SDK-Enforced Schema]] — Decision (akira, 2026-09): keep enrichment on plain text generation plus a deterministic sanitize/validate chain instead of SDK-enforced structured output, and amend the requirement that claimed API-layer enforcement. None of the three deciding criteria was measured, measuring token cost would mean half-building the alternative, and the rewording itself had to name each path's real mechanism, because every uniform claim (including the corrections) turned out false.
- [[GATE Generative Active Task Elicitation]] — GATE has a language model interview the user, with open-ended questions, yes/no questions or generated edge cases to label, so the user's preferences are captured better than by writing a prompt.
- [[GCG Adversarial Suffix Attack]] — GCG appends an optimised token suffix to a harmful request by gradient-guided greedy search so an aligned model starts with an affirmative reply; suffixes tuned on open models transferred to some closed models in mid-2023 tests, and the paper also introduced the AdvBench harmful-behaviour set.
- [[GeM-CoT]] — Routes each question to a typed demonstration pool by embedding similarity, falls back to zero-shot CoT for unmatched questions, and grows the pool by clustering cached fallbacks into auto-built demonstrations.
- [[GEPA Reflective Prompt Evolution]] — GEPA is a prompt optimizer for multi-module LLM systems that has a reflection model read run traces and textual feedback, rewrite one module's prompt, and keep candidates on a per-instance Pareto frontier; the paper reports it beating GRPO on five of six tasks with far fewer rollouts (self-reported).
- [[Graded-Uncertainty Enrichment Contract]] — An LLM annotation schema that separates three levels of not-knowing — non-blocking `uncertainty_flags`, a usable-but-incomplete `enrichment_status=partial`, and a blocking `needs_review` with a reason — so sparse input lowers certainty rather than quality scores, enums carry `unknown` while multi-selects use empty arrays, and the downstream gate routes `partial_but_usable` items to production instead of a review backlog.
- [[Graph of Thoughts]] — Prompting framework where a user-written Graph of Operations (Generate, Aggregate, Score, KeepBest, Repeat) lets thoughts merge and loop; the paper reports lower sorting error than Tree of Thoughts at lower cost on GPT-3.5 (self-reported).
- [[HiAR-ICL Thought Cards]] — HiAR-ICL replaces worked examples in the prompt with abstract thought cards, reasoning-action chains found offline by Monte-Carlo tree search on 200 seed questions and retrieved by problem complexity; the authors report 80.6% on MATH500 with a 7B model and about 7 to 22 times less time than rStar (self-reported).
- [[Hypotheses-to-Theories (HtT)]] — Two-stage prompting that induces a library of rules from training questions, filters them by coverage and confidence, then prepends the library so the model retrieves rules rather than generating them.
- [[Implicit Weight-Patch Theory of In-Context Learning]] — Dherin et al. show algebraically that the effect of a prompt on a transformer block equals a rank-1 patch of the first MLP weight matrix, plus a bias patch with skip connections; the equivalence is exact but shown only on toy models.
- [[Inverse Scaling in Test-Time Compute]] — Gema et al. find tasks where giving a reasoning model a longer budget lowers accuracy, with distractor, spurious-feature and constraint-tracking failure modes, so test-time compute is not monotone.
- [[Iterated Decomposition]] — A human-in-the-loop loop for building select-then-generate LM pipelines: run on gold answers, inspect failing steps in a trace viewer, fix the worst one (usually paragraph selection) and repeat; reported gains on three science Q&A tasks with text-davinci-002 (self-reported).
- [[Jailbreak Rapid Response]] — Jailbreak rapid response adapts an input guard after seeing a few successful jailbreaks, multiplying each with generated variants; RapidResponseBench tests it on six attack strategies and reports large attack-success drops from fine-tuning the guard (self-reported).
- [[Least-to-Most Prompting]] — Two-stage few-shot prompting that first lists easier subproblems and then solves them in order, feeding earlier answers forward; large gains on length generalisation, small ones on GSM8K.
- [[Length Limits Are a Fit Layer, Not a Prompt]] — Word and character limits for layout-bound generation are a deterministic problem — let the model write, enforce `maxLength` (characters, not words) in the schema, truncate or reflow in code after generation, and cap the validate-and-retry loop at 2–3 attempts before falling back to deterministic repair, because prompt-stated limits fail 5–20% of the time and retry error messages drift.
- [[LLM Action Grounding for Embodied Planning]] — Two frozen-LLM planners for household tasks, one translating each generated step to the nearest admissible action and one retrieving examples and re-planning on failure; both report executability or low-data gains, with a correctness cost in the first (self-reported).
- [[LLM Reasoning Surveys and the Can-LLMs-Reason Debate]] — Four sources that map or question LLM reasoning without new benchmark runs of their own: a maths-reasoning survey, a regime-by-architecture survey, a prompting/architecture/learning survey, and Kambhampati's argument that LLMs retrieve approximately rather than plan.
- [[LM-BFF and PET Prompt-Based Fine-Tuning]] — Two 2021 recipes that recast classification as a cloze task for a small masked language model and fine-tune on few labels (LM-BFF 16 per class, PET 32 in total): LM-BFF (RoBERTa-large, searched templates, sampled demonstrations) and PET (ALBERT, ensembled patterns distilled into a classifier); each reports its own gains (self-reported).
- [[LM-Guided Chain-of-Thought]] — A small fine-tuned LM (FLAN-T5 80M) writes the rationale and a frozen larger LM (FLAN-T5 XXL 11B) answers; distilled rationales beat plain CoT on 2WikiMultiHopQA by about 10% EM/F1, while the RL stage adds only tenths of a point (self-reported).
- [[Logic-of-Thought]] — A zero-shot augmentation that extracts propositions from the context, expands them with logic laws in Python, translates the result to English and appends it to the original context; improved the baseline in 37 of 40 comparisons in the paper (self-reported).
- [[Logits Masking and Grammar-Constrained Generation]] — Two decode-time patterns from a design-patterns book: logits masking zeroes rule-violating continuations at sampling, grammar restricts output to a context-free syntax; try-and-try-again is only cheap when the failure rate is under about 10 percent.
- [[Logprobs as Confidence Signal]] — Where the API exposes token log-probabilities they are the best confidence instrument — gate on average token probability, read classification probabilities off a first token, give every label a distinct first token to avoid prefix collision, and calibrate per-label offsets on labelled data.
- [[Long-Context Prompting]] — Ordering and grounding rules for long prompts — recency bias plus lost-in-the-middle create a Valley of Meh, so frame with an introduction, put longform data next, refocus the question at the end, transition into the answer, and ground in extracted quotes.
- [[LongICLBench and Many-Shot ICL Scaling]] — Two 2024 studies of in-context learning with hundreds to thousands of demonstrations. Many-shot ICL keeps improving on classification up to about 1,000 examples, while LongICLBench shows most models collapse when the label space is large (self-reported).
- [[MAKER Million-Step Decomposition]] — MAKER solved a 20-disk Towers of Hanoi (over a million steps) with gpt-4.1-mini by decomposing into one-step agents, voting first-to-ahead-by-k per step and discarding malformed answers, with a theoretical cost of order s ln s (self-reported).
- [[Medprompt]] — Medprompt combines nearest-neighbour few-shot exemplars, self-generated chain-of-thought and a choice-shuffle ensemble to push GPT-4 past specialist medical models on multiple-choice benchmarks.
- [[MetaCrit Adaptive Critique]] — A four-call pipeline (generator, read-only monitor, controller, synthesizer) that scales critique to the answer instead of always critiquing; in its paper, appending a plain critique instruction to every baseline made accuracy collapse, while the graded pipeline reached 94.12 on TruthfulQA with GPT-3.5-Turbo (self-reported).
- [[Mixtures of In-Context Learners]] — MoICL splits demonstrations into subsets, runs the model on each as an expert, and mixes their next-token log-probabilities with learned weights, including negative ones; it beat concatenated ICL on 5 of 7 classification sets and resisted noisy or imbalanced demonstrations (self-reported).
- [[Multi-Expert Prompting]] — One prompt chain simulates several experts, answers as each, then merges the answers with seven subtasks borrowed from the Nominal Group Technique; the paper reports 89.35 True% on TruthfulQA with ChatGPT at about 22.7 times the tokens of zero-shot CoT, and no gain on reasoning multiple choice (self-reported).
- [[Multimodal Chain-of-Thought]] — A two-stage recipe for sub-1B T5 models: stage one writes a rationale from text plus ViT image features, stage two reads the rationale and answers; the paper reports 90.45% on ScienceQA with 738M parameters and traces text-only failures to hallucinated rationales (self-reported).
- [[Outlines Guided Generation]] — Outlines casts constrained generation as a finite-state machine over the vocabulary, precomputing an index from state to allowed tokens so each step applies a logit mask at roughly constant cost; it guarantees format, not correctness, and the paper's evidence is thin.
- [[Overthinking and Concise Reasoning]] — Longer reasoning is not better reasoning: o1-like models spend thousands of extra tokens on easy questions after a correct first answer, and several methods (self-training, length caps, token-complexity thresholds) trade a small accuracy loss for large token savings.
- [[PAIR Black-Box Jailbreak Refinement]] — PAIR has an attacker LLM rewrite a jailbreak prompt over a few rounds using the target's reply and a judge's score; the paper reports tens of queries per success on some targets, weak results on heavily safety-tuned ones, and better transfer than GCG (self-reported).
- [[PEZ Gradient-Based Hard Prompt Discovery]] — PEZ finds discrete text prompts by gradient descent on a continuous iterate that is projected to the nearest token embeddings at each forward pass; it needs white-box gradients, yields often unreadable prompts, and was tested only on small models and narrow classification.
- [[PGD Adversarial Training]] — Adversarial robustness cast as a min-max problem: the inner step attacks each input with multi-step projected gradient descent and the outer step trains on those worst cases; the paper reports robust MNIST and partly robust CIFAR10 classifiers (self-reported), for images only.
- [[Plan-and-Solve Prompting]] — Zero-shot prompt (PS and PS+) that asks the model to devise a plan, extract variables and compute intermediate results before answering; reported on text-davinci-003 to beat Zero-shot-CoT on math sets with triggers tuned per dataset (self-reported).
- [[Principled Instructions (26 Prompt Principles)]] — A list of 26 hand-written prompting rules in five categories, tested on a 20-questions-per-principle human-rated benchmark; the paper's gains are self-reported win rates without statistics, and the politeness, tipping and threat rules were never tested individually.
- [[Program-Aided Language Models (PAL)]] — The model writes a program and an interpreter computes the answer — few-shot examples are code with the reasoning in comments, so exact computation (dates, arithmetic) moves out of the token predictor; only safe with a sandbox, and today usually a code-execution tool.
- [[Prompt Assembly as Snippet Packing]] — The feedforward pass of an LLM app (retrieve → snippetize → score → assemble) ends in a packing problem — pick snippets by priority under a token budget, honouring elastic versions and mutual exclusion, then emit the winners in document order.
- [[Prompt Chaining]] — Decomposing a complex task into simpler prompts with fixed I/O contracts — trading a general/weak prompt for strong single-job steps wired as a pipeline or (by default) a DAG; the boundary case between prompting and harness engineering.
